), indicating feature diversity is higher with absolute value activation.

Author Contributions

GY developed the core theory and ran experiments in early part of the exploratory stage and most experiments in the final draft.
DY worked on and proved key claims for linear resnets (including the limiting equations, convergence, and classification of parametrization), drafted the very first version of the paper, and ran experiments verifying the theoretical claims (including the convergence of linear case and feature diversity separation).
CZ ran experiments in later part of the exploratory stage. They revealed the viability of Depth-
\mu
P in the block depth 1 case, in contrast to the general block depth case.
CZ also ran the Megatron experiments in the final version of the paper.
SH contributed to brainstorming since the beginning of the project, wrote the warm-up section on linear networks, formalized the notion of feature diversity exponent, and helped transforming experimental results into plots and visualizations.

Appendix A
Notations

This section provides an introduction to the new TP notations from
[
24
]
. We only require the definition of the inner and outer products in this paper.

Averaging over
n

When
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we always use greek subscript
\alpha,\beta,\ldots\in[n]
to index its entries.
Then
\langle x_{\alpha}\rangle_{\alpha}
denotes its average entry.
This notation will only be used to average over
n
-dimensions, but not over constant dimensions.

A.1
The Tensor Program Ansatz: Representing Vectors via Random Variables

From the Tensor Programs framework
[
25
]
, we know that as width becomes large, the entries of the (pre-)activation
vectors and their gradients will become roughly iid, both at initialization and training. Hence any such vector’s behavior can be
tracked via a random variable that reflects the distribution of its
entries. While we call this the “Tensor Program Ansatz”, it is a completely rigorous calculus.

A.1.1
Ket Notation

Concretely, if
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is one such vector, then we write
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle\in\mathbb{R}
(called a
ket
)
for such a random variable, such that
x
’s entries look like iid
samples from
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle
.For any two such vectors
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
(x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
for each
\alpha
will look like iid samples from the random vector
(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle)
, such that, for example,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{x^{\top}y}{n}=\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle\cdot\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle
, which we write succinctly as just
\langle x\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle
.
Here
\langle x\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
is called a
bra
, interpreted as a sort of “transpose” to
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle
.
In our convention,
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle
is always a random variable independent of
n
and
x
always has
\Theta(1)
typical entry size.
26
26

26

i.e.,
\|x\|^{2}/n=\Theta(1)
as
n\to\infty
.

This notation can be generalized to the case where
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k},\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times j}
. In this case, we can think of
\langle\bm{x}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{y}\rangle
as the
k\times j
matrix given by
(\langle x_{a}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y_{b}\rangle)_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq a\leq k\\
1\leq b\leq j\end{subarray}}
.

Because we will often need to multiply a ket with a diagonal matrix, we introduce a shorthand:

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle_{\bm{\chi}}=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\mathrm{Diag}(\bm{\chi}),

(7)

if
\bm{x}
is
n\times k
and
\bm{\chi}
is a
k
-dimensional vector.

A.1.2
Outer Product

Likewise, if both
\bm{x}
and
\bm{y}
have shape
n\times k
, the expression

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\ \text{ represents the limit of $\bm{x}\bm{y}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}$.}

More formally,

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
is defined as an operator that takes a ket
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle\in\mathbb{R}^{j}

and return the ket

(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}})\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle(\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle)\in\mathbb{R}^{j}

i.e., it returns the random vector
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
multiplied
by the deterministic matrix
\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times j}

on the right.
This corresponds to the limit of
\bm{x}\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{z}/n
.
Likewise,
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
acts on a bra
\langle\bm{w}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\in\mathbb{R}^{j}

by

\langle\bm{w}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}})=(\langle\bm{w}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle)\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\in\mathbb{R}^{j}.

which corresponds to the limit of
\frac{1}{n}\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{x}\bm{y}^{\top}
.
This definition of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
makes the expressions

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle,\quad\langle\bm{w}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}},\quad\langle\bm{w}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle

unambiguous (since any way of ordering the operations give the same
answer).

Remark A.1

(Potential Confusion)
.

One should
not
interpret
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
as the scalar random variable
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\cdot\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{y}\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x^{i}\rangle\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y^{i}\rangle
, which would act on a ket
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle
to produce
(\langle\bm{x}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\cdot\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}})\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle=\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\cdot\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{y}\rangle)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle
, which is deterministic.
On the other hand,
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle
is always a linear combination of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle
, a nondeterministic random variable in general.
In particular, any correlation between
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle
and
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{y}\rangle
does not directly play a role in their outer product
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
: we always have
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}
, where
(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{y}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}},\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}})
is an iid copy of
(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{y}\rangle,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle)
independent from
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle
.

Outer Product with Diagonal Inserted

Finally, if
\bm{\chi}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
is deterministic, then (consistent with
eq.

7
) we define
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle{}_{\bm{\chi}}\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}

as the operator that acts on kets
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle\in\mathbb{R}^{j}
by

(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle{}_{\bm{\chi}}\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}})\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle{}_{\bm{\chi}}\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\bm{z}}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\mathrm{Diag}(\bm{\chi})(\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle)\in\mathbb{R}^{j}.

Morally,
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle{}_{\bm{\chi}}\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
is just a shorter way of writing

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\mathrm{Diag}(\bm{\chi})\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
and represents the limit of
\bm{x}\mathrm{Diag}(\bm{\chi})\bm{y}^{\top}
.
In particular,
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle{}_{\bm{1}}\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
.

A.1.3
Nonlinear Outer Product

If
xy^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is the (linear) outer product of two vectors
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then
\phi(xy^{\top})
, the entrywise application of nonlinear
\phi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
to
xy^{\top}
, is a kind of
nonlinear outer product
.Passing to the ket notation, in general we define
\phi\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\right)

as the operator that acts on kets as

\phi\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\right)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}$}}\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\phi\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x^{i}\rangle\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y^{i}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\right)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}

where
\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y^{1}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}},\ldots,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y^{k}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}},\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\right)

is an iid copy of
\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y^{1}\rangle,\ldots,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y^{k}\rangle,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle\right)

independent from
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle
and the expectation is taken only over
the former.
This is just like, in the finite
n
case,

\phi\left(\bm{x}\bm{y}^{\top}\right)\bm{z}/n=\phi\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}x^{i}{y^{i\top}}\right){\bm{z}}/n.

Moreover, if
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{w}\rangle\in\mathbb{R}^{j},\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
then

\displaystyle\langle\bm{w}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\phi\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\right)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{w}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\phi\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\right)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{j\times k}

\displaystyle=\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\phi\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x^{i}\rangle\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y^{i}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\right)\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{w}\rangle\otimes\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\right)

where
\otimes
denotes outer product of vectors and expectation
is taken over everything.

More generally, if
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{t}\to\mathbb{R}
, then
\phi\Big(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}_{1}\rangle\langle\bm{y}_{1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}},\ldots,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}_{t}\rangle\langle\bm{y}_{t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\Big)
is an operator taking kets to kets, defined by

\displaystyle\phi\Big(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}_{1}\rangle\langle\bm{y}_{1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}},\ldots,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}_{t}\rangle\langle\bm{y}_{t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\Big)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}$}}\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\phi\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{i}\rangle\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y_{1}^{i}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}},\ldots,\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{i}\rangle\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y_{t}^{i}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}\right)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{z}\rangle^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\displaystyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 1.0pt\framebox{$\textstyle 1$}\kern 1.0pt}{\kern 0.7pt\framebox{$\scriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.7pt}{\kern 0.5pt\framebox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}\kern 0.5pt}}}

Remark A.2

(Potential Confusion)
.

Note
\phi(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}})
is not the image of the operator
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\langle\bm{y}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
under
\phi
in the continuous function calculus of operators, but rather a “coordinatewise application” of
\phi
.
For example, if
\phi(t)=t^{2}
, then
\phi(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle\langle y\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}})

is not

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle\langle y\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle\langle y\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
, the latter being what typically “squaring an operator” means, but rather
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle^{2}\langle y\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}^{2}=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\odot x\rangle\langle y\odot y\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
.

A.1.4
Comparison with Previous
Z^{\bullet}
Notation

For readers familiar with the
Tensor Programs
papers, this new “bra-ket” notation
(aka Dirac notation) relates to the old
Z^{\bullet}
notation by

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle=Z^{x},\quad\langle x\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle=\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}Z^{x}Z^{y}.

The new notation’s succinctness of expectation inner product should already be apparent.
Furthermore, the old notation is not very compatible with multi-vectors whereas
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle
makes it clear that
\rangle
represents the constant dimension side.
Consequently, (nonlinear) outer product is awkward to express in it, especially when its contraction with random variables requires an explicit expectation symbol
\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}
.

Appendix B
Infinite-Width Limit with the Bra-ket notation

As before, when the width
n
of the program goes to infinity, one can infer how the program behaves via a calculus of random variables.
We define them below via the new ket notation instead of the earlier
Z
notation.

Ket Construction.

We recursively define the random variable
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle
(called a
ket
) for each vector
x
and deterministic number
\mathring{\theta}
for each scalar
\theta
in the program.
For a vector
Wx
in the program, we also define random variables
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
and
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
(called
hat-ket
and
dot-ket
respectively) such that
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
. These are the same as
\widehat{Z}
and
\dot{Z}
in the old TP notation
[
25
]
and they satisfy

Hat

All hat-kets
are jointly Gaussian with zero-mean and covariance
27
27

27

In
eq.

8
,
\mathbb{I}(W=U)
is the deterministic number that is 1 iff
W
and
U
are the same matrix (as symbols in the program) and 0 otherwise. This should
not
be interpreted as a random variable that is 1 precisely when
W
and
U
take the same values.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Uy\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}(W=U)\langle x\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle

(8)

Dot

Every dot-ket is a linear combination of previous kets, expressed by the following equation

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}$}}\sum_{y\in\bm{x}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{\top}y\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}

(9)

eq.

9
is the same equation as in
[
25
, Zdot]

but formulated much more succinctly in the bra-ket notation:

\displaystyle\text{\cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{yang2022tensor}{}{}, Zdot]}},\quad\dot{Z}^{Wx}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in\bm{x}}Z^{y}\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\frac{\partial Z^{x}}{\partial\widehat{Z}^{W^{\top}y}}.

There is an alternative notion for
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
in
Yang and Littwin 2023
that write

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bm{x}\rangle\check{\langle}W^{\top}\bm{x}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle.

This is more convenient to write as we introduce the operator view.

We can see the ket
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}Wx\rangle
as the result of the action of an operator on the ket
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle
.

Definition B.1
.

Let
W
be an initial matrix in a Tensor Program.
We define
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}},\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}},\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}
to be the linear operators on kets

28
28

28

To be rigorous, we need to specify the “Hilbert space” of kets.
This is somewhat pedantic and not crucial to the key points of this paper, but the Hilbert space can be constructed as follows:
Let
\sigma(\pi)
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the kets of the program
\pi
.
Let
\Sigma(\pi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}$}}\bigcup_{\pi^{\prime}\supseteq\pi}\sigma(\pi)
be the union (more precisely, the direct limit) of
\sigma(\pi^{\prime})
over all programs
\pi^{\prime}
extending
\pi
.
Then the Hilbert space in question is the
L^{2}
space of random variables over the
\Sigma
of our program.

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x\rangle

Parameter Update

In the SGD case, the parameter update of
W^{l}
is simple. With the operator notation and outer product notation, we can write

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{t+1}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{t}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}-\eta\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h^{l}_{t}\rangle{}_{\chi_{t}}\langle x^{l-1}_{t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}.

In this work,
\Delta
denotes change for one step, i.e.,

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\Delta W_{t+1}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=-\eta\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h^{l}_{t}\rangle{}_{\chi_{t}}\langle x^{l-1}_{t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}};

\bar{\Delta}
denotes total change, i.e.,

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=-\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\eta\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h^{l}_{\tau}\rangle{}_{\chi_{\tau}}\langle x^{l-1}_{\tau}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}},

which we write succinctly
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=-\eta\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}\bm{h}^{l}_{<t}\rangle{}_{\bm{\chi}}\langle\bm{x}^{l-1}_{<t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
. (Compared to
Yang and Littwin 2023
,
\Delta
and
\bar{\Delta}
are changed from
\delta
and
\Delta
because we want to use
\delta
for gradients instead of

, which is now used for depth differentiation).

Note in the general case,

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\Delta W_{t+1}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=-\eta\overline{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}\bm{h}^{l}_{\leq t}\rangle{}_{\bm{\chi}_{\leq t}}\langle\bm{x}^{l-1}_{\leq t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}

where

\overline{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}\bm{h}^{l}_{\leq t}\rangle{}_{\bm{\chi}_{\leq t}}\langle\bm{x}^{l-1}_{\leq t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}Q_{t}^{l}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h^{l}_{0}\rangle{}_{\chi_{0}}\langle x^{l-1}_{0}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}},\ldots,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h^{l}_{t}\rangle{}_{\chi_{t}}\langle x^{l-1}_{t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}).

So

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=-\eta\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}\overline{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}\bm{h}^{l}_{\leq\tau}\rangle{}_{\bm{\chi}_{\leq\tau}}\langle\bm{x}^{l-1}_{\leq\tau}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}.

Appendix C
Details of the linear case

C.1
Proof sketch of
4.2

Here we provide a proof sketch of
4.2
, the formal prove is implied by the existence of
\Gamma
and
C
in the infinite depth limit.

Proof sketch.

The claims can be reasoned by induction on
t
and
l
. Let us take
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle
as an example, since
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{t}^{l-1}\rangle
is symmetric with
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle
.
By expanding the definition of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle
, we have

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l-1}\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{t}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s}^{l}\rangle\left(\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l-1}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s}^{l}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\langle x_{s}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l-1}\rangle\right).

Note by induction,
\langle x_{s}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l-1}\rangle=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
\frac{\partial x_{t}^{l-1}}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s}^{l}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}=\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{L})
,
so

\displaystyle\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\talloblong$}x_{t}^{l}\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l-1}\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{t}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s}^{l}\rangle

\displaystyle=\xi_{t}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U\rangle+\sum_{m=1}^{l}\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m}x_{t}^{m-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{l}\sum_{s^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\rangle.

Then by unwinding
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\rangle
and noting that by induction,
\forall s<t
,
\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m}x_{s}^{m-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\right)
,

\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s}^{m}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\right)
,

\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U\rangle}=\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)
,

\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}nV\rangle}=\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)
,
we have

\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m}x_{s}^{m-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\right),\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s}^{m}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\right),\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U\rangle}=\mathcal{O}\left(1\right),\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}nV\rangle}=\mathcal{O}\left(1\right).

Also by unwinding,
\forall\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle\in\{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{s}^{m}\rangle,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s}^{m}\rangle\}
,

\displaystyle\langle y\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m^{\prime}}\sum_{s^{\prime}}\sum_{t^{\prime}}\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m^{\prime}}x_{t^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m^{\prime}}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}\cdot\langle x_{t^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}-1}\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{m^{\prime}}\sum_{s^{\prime}}\sum_{t^{\prime}}\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m^{\prime}\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{t^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m^{\prime}\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}\cdot\langle\widetilde{\delta}x_{t^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{s^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U\rangle}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U\rangle}+\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}nV\rangle}\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}y\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}nV\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1).\qed

C.2
Formal recursive formula of
\Gamma
and
C

By the same way of expanding
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle
and
\langle y\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{l}\rangle
, we formally derive the recursive formula for
\Gamma
and
C
below.

Lemma C.1

(Finite depth recursive formula for
\Gamma
and
C
)
.

\Gamma
can be computed recursively as follows:

For
t=0,\ldots,T-1
,

•

\forall q\in(0,1],\Gamma_{t,-1,0,q}(0,q)=\xi_{t}
,

•

For
l=1,\ldots,L
,

\forall r\leq t
,

\forall p\in\left(\frac{l-1}{L},\frac{l}{L}\right]
,

\forall q\in(0,1]
,
\forall b\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle C_{t,s,0}(p)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\Gamma_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},q\right)C_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},q\right)\mathrm{d}q;

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma_{t,r,0,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},q\right)+\mathbb{I}_{[(t=r)\wedge(b=0)\wedge(l=\lceil Lq\rceil)]}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,r,1,b}\left(\frac{l}{L},q\right)\left(\Gamma_{t,s,0,1}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},\frac{l}{L}\right)-C_{t,s,0}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right)\right).

•

\mathring{f}_{t}=\Gamma_{t,-1,0,1}(1,1)
,

•

\mathring{\chi}_{t}=\ell^{\prime}_{t}(\mathring{f}_{t})
,

•

\forall q\in(0,1],\Gamma_{t,-1,1,1}(1,q)=\mathring{\chi}_{t}
,

•

For
l=L,\ldots,1
,

\forall r\leq t
,

\forall p\in\left(\frac{l-2}{L},\frac{l-1}{L}\right]
,

\forall q\in(0,1]
,
\forall b\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle C_{t,s,1}\left(p+\frac{1}{L}\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\Gamma_{t,t^{\prime},1,b}(l/L,q)C_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},1,b}(l/L,q)\mathrm{d}q;

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,1,b}\left(p,q\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma_{t,r,1,b}\left(\frac{l}{L},q\right)+\mathbb{I}_{[(t=r)\wedge(b=1)\wedge(l=\lceil Lq\rceil)]}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,r,0,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},q\right)\left(\Gamma_{t,s,1,0}\left(\frac{l}{L},\frac{l}{L}\right)-C_{t,s,1}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right)\right).

The proof is straightforward from Program
1
. The recursive nature of
\Gamma
and
C
yields the following infinite-depth behavior.

Proposition C.2

(Infinite depth limit of
\Gamma
and
C
)
.

In the limit
L\to\infty
, we have
\forall p\in[0,1],q\in(0,1],b\in\{0,1\}
:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,-1,0,0}(0,q)=\xi_{t};

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}(p,q)=\mathbb{I}_{[(t=r)\wedge(b=0)\wedge(p\geq q)]}+\int_{0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,r,1,b}(p^{\prime},q)\cdot(\Gamma_{t,s,0,1}(p^{\prime},p^{\prime})-C_{t,s,0}(p^{\prime}))\mathrm{d}p^{\prime};

\displaystyle\mathring{f}_{t}=\Gamma_{t,-1,0,1}(1,1);

\displaystyle\mathring{\chi}_{t}=\ell^{\prime}_{t}(\mathring{f}_{t});

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,-1,1,1}(1,q)=\mathring{\chi}_{t};

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,1,b}(p,q)=\mathbb{I}_{[(t=r)\wedge(b=1)\wedge(p\leq q)]}+\int_{p}^{1}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,r,0,b}(p^{\prime},q)\cdot(\Gamma_{t,s,1,0}(p^{\prime},p^{\prime})-C_{t,s,1}(p^{\prime}))\mathrm{d}p^{\prime};

\displaystyle C_{t,s,a}(p)=\sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\Gamma_{t,t^{\prime},a,b}(p,q)C_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},a,b}(p,q)\mathrm{d}q.

C.3
Convergence of
\Gamma
and
C
when
L=2^{k}

In this section, we prove
\Gamma
and
C
will converge when
L\to\infty
. For convenience, we will only consider the case when
L=2^{k}
for some integer
k
. To distinguish
\Gamma
and
C
corresponding to different
L
, we add the depth as the superscript, i.e.,
\Gamma^{L}
and
C^{L}
.

Theorem C.3
.

\forall t\leq T,s<t,a\in\{0,1\},b\in\{0,1\}
,
\forall p\in[0,1],q\in(0,1]
,

•

\{\Gamma^{2^{k}}_{t,s,a,b}(p,q)\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence,

•

\{C^{2^{k}}_{t,s,a}(p)\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence.

The proof is by induction on
t
