. We will prove the following claims (A) (B) (C) (D) on
t>0
given they are satisfied for any
s<t
. For
t=0
, (A) (B) (C) (D) are trivial.

Assumption on
s<t

Assume
\exists c>1
such that
\forall L>L^{\prime}
and
L=2^{k}
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\forall s<t
,
\forall r<s
,

(A)

\forall p\in\{0,\frac{1}{L},\ldots,1\},q\in(0,1]
,

|\Gamma^{L/2}_{s,r,a,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,a,b}(p,q)|\leq c/L,\qquad|C^{L/2}_{s,r,a}(p,q)-C^{L}_{s,r,a}(p,q)|\leq c/L.

(B)

|\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,a,b}(p,q)|\leq c
,
|C^{L}_{s,r,a}(p)|\leq c
.

(C)

C^{L}_{s,r,a}(p)
is
c
-Lipschitz w.r.t.
p
, and
\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,a,b}(p,q)
is
c
-Lipschitz w.r.t.
p
.

(D)

|\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,0,1}(p-\frac{1}{L},p+\frac{1}{L})-\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,0,1}(p-\frac{1}{L},p)|\leq c/L
,

|\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,0}(p,p)-\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,0}(p,p-\frac{1}{L})|\leq c/L
.

Remark

(A) indicates that
\{\Gamma^{2^{k}}_{s,r,a,b}\}_{k}
and
\{C^{2^{k}}_{s,r,a}\}_{k}
converge.
We only care about
r<s
because
C^{L}_{s,s,a}
will never be used, and
\Gamma^{L}_{s,s,a,b}
is known: for
p\in\{0,\frac{1}{L},\ldots,1\}
,

\Gamma^{L}_{s,s,a,b}(p,q)=\mathbb{I}[(a=0)\wedge(b=0)\wedge(p\geq q)]+\mathbb{I}[(a=1)\wedge(b=1)\wedge(p+1/L\leq q)].

Proof for
t
-th step (the forward pass)

In the following subsections, we will prove inductively on increasing order of all
L>L^{\prime}
and
L=2^{k}
, and increasing order of
p\in\{0,1/L,\ldots,1\}
that
\forall s<t
,

(D0)

|\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}(p,p+\frac{2}{L})-\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}(p,p+\frac{1}{L})|\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p)/L
;

(C0)

For
s<t
,

|\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,b}(p-\frac{1}{L},q)|\leq tcc_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L
;

(B0)

|\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,b}(p,q)|\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{2L}))
;

(A0)

|\Gamma^{L/2}_{t,s,0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,b}(p,q)|\leq c_{3}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{2L}))/L
;

(C1)

|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p)|\leq c_{4}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L
;

(B1)

|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})|\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p)
;

(A1)

|C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})|\leq c_{5}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p)/L,

where
c_{2}=\max\{\xi_{t}^{2},|\xi_{t}|\}\exp(c_{1}/{2L^{\prime}})
,
c_{3}=3ct
,
c_{4}=4t(t+1)c^{2}+2tc
,
c_{5}=c_{4}+1
,
c_{1}=c^{3}t(4ct+2c_{4}+29)+tc(3c_{4}+14)+c(2c_{4}+2c)
.

Proof for
t
-th step (the backward pass)

Similar bounds also apply to
\Gamma_{t,s,1,b}
and
C_{t,s,1}
by induction on decreasing order of
p
.

Conclusion

Combining both backward pass and forward pass at time
t
shows (A)(B)(C)(D) also hold for
s=t
with a larger (but constant)
c
. Thus, (A)(B)(C)(D) hold for any constant
s
by induction on training steps.

C.3.1
\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,b}(p,q)
in forward pass (Proof for D0, C0, B0, A0)

We first consider

\displaystyle\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},q\right)+\mathbb{I}[(t=r)\wedge(b=0)\wedge(Lp=\lceil Lq\rceil)]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p,q\right)\left(\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},p\right)-C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p\right)\right).

(D0) Difference between
\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}(p,p+\frac{2}{L})
and
\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}(p,p+\frac{1}{L})

Assume
p\geq 1/L
(
p=0
is trivial),
let
q=p+1/L,q^{\prime}=p+2/L
, note that
\Gamma_{s,s,1,b}^{L}(p,q)=\Gamma_{s,s,1,b}^{L}(p,q^{\prime})
since
p+1/L\leq q\leq q^{\prime}
, so for
r<t
,

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q^{\prime}\right)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},q\right)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},q^{\prime}\right)|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}|\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p,q\right)-\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p,q^{\prime}\right)|\left|\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},p\right)-C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L+\frac{1}{L}\cdot t\cdot c/L\cdot 2c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+2ct/L)c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p)/L,

as
c_{1}\geq 2ct
.

(C0) Lipschitz w.r.t.
p

For
r<t
,

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}(p-\frac{1}{L},q)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p,q\right)\left(\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},p\right)-C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p\right)\right)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}c\left(c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))+c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ ctc_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L.

(B0) Bounded

Again assume
p\geq 1/L
(
p=0
is trivial because
c_{2}\geq|\xi_{t}|\exp(c_{1}/2L)
), since
|\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}(p-\frac{1}{L},q)|\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))
,
we can bound
|\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}(p,q)|
:

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}(p,q)|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))+ctc_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))(1+ct/L)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{2L})),

as long as
c_{1}\geq 2ct
.

(A0) Difference between
L
and
L/2
bounded

When
p=0
, it is trivial.
When
p=1/L
, it is also trivial by Lipschitz w.r.t.
p
, which results

|\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L/2}\left(p,q\right)-\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L}\left(p,q\right)|\leq 3ctc_{2}/L\leq c_{3}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}/2L)/L.

When
p\geq 2/L
, since

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L/2}\left(p,q\right)=\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L/2}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},q\right)+\frac{2}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,r,1,b}^{L/2}\left(p,q\right)\left(\Gamma_{t,s,0,1}^{L/2}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p\right)-C_{t,s,0}^{L/2}(p)\right),

we compare it with
\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L}\left(p,q\right)
expanded based on its previous two steps

\displaystyle\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},q\right)+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p,q\right)\left(\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},p\right)-C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},q\right)\left(\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p-\frac{1}{L}\right)-C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p-\frac{1}{L}\right)\right).

In order to bridge the two above, namely matching the inputs for
\Gamma
and
C
, we need a middle term

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},q\right)+\frac{2}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p,q\right)\left(\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p\right)-C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p\right)\right).

Now we can bound
|\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)-\widetilde{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)|
, and
|\widetilde{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)-\Gamma^{L/2}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)|
separately, which add up to be the bound for
|\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)-\Gamma^{L/2}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)|
.

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\Gamma^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)-\widetilde{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,r,0,b}\left(p,q\right)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}|\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p,q\right)|\left|\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},p\right)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\left|\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p-\frac{1}{L},q\right)\left(\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p-\frac{1}{L}\right)-C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p-\frac{1}{L}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.-\Gamma^{L}_{s,r,1,b}\left(p,q\right)\left(\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p\right)-C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{L}\cdot ct\cdot ctc_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))/L+\frac{1}{L}\cdot 2t\cdot c/L\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\cdot ct\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))/L+\frac{1}{L}\cdot ct\cdot c_{4}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))/L

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c^{2}t^{2}+3ct+c_{4}ct}{L^{2}}\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L})).

and

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L/2}\left(p,q\right)-\widetilde{\Gamma}_{t,r,0,b}^{L}\left(p,q\right)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L/2}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},q\right)-\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},q\right)|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}c\left|\Gamma_{t,s,0,1}^{L/2}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p\right)-\Gamma_{t,s,0,1}^{L}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p\right)-C_{t,s,0}^{L/2}\left(p\right)+C_{t,s,0}^{L}\left(p\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\frac{c}{L}\left(|\Gamma^{L}_{t,s,0,1}\left(p-\frac{2}{L},p\right)|+|C^{L}_{t,s,0}\left(p\right)|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{L}\cdot(c_{3}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))+ct(c_{3}+c_{5})c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L+2t\cdot\frac{c}{L}\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L})))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c_{3}+ct(c_{3}+c_{5}+2)/L}{L}\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L})).

In sum, as
c_{1}\geq\frac{2(c_{3}+c_{5}+ct+c_{4}+5)}{3}
,

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L/2}\left(p,q\right)-\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}^{L}\left(p,q\right)|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c_{3}+ct(c_{3}+c_{5}+ct+c_{4}+5)/L}{L}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{2L}))/L.

C.3.2
C_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})
in forward pass (Proof for C1, B1, A1)

Now consider
C^{L}_{t,s,0}
. By expanding

C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})=\sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)C^{L}_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)\mathrm{d}q,

we will have

\displaystyle C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})=

\displaystyle\sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)C^{L}_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle+\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\int_{0}^{p}C^{L}_{t,s^{\prime},0}(q)\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}(p,q)\mathrm{d}q.

(C1) Lipschitz

Since
C^{L}_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}
and
\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}
are bounded and Lipschitz,

\displaystyle|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}|\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{1}{L},q)|\cdot c^{2}\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle+\sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}|\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{1}{L},q)|\cdot c\cdot\frac{c}{L}\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle+\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{1}{L}\cdot|C^{L}_{t,s^{\prime},0}(p)\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}(p,p)|

\displaystyle+\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\int_{0}^{p-\frac{1}{L}}|C^{L}_{t,s^{\prime},0}(q)|\cdot\frac{c}{L}\mathrm{d}q.

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ 1/L\cdot(2t(s+1)\cdot ctc_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))\cdot c^{2}+2t(s+1)c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))\cdot c^{2}

\displaystyle+s\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))\cdot c+s\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))\cdot c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (4t(s+1)c^{2}+2sc)/L\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{4}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L.

(B1) Bounded

Since
|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p)|\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))
, we bound
C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})
as:

|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})|\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))\cdot(1+c_{4}/L)\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p),

as long as
c_{1}\geq c_{4}
.

(A1) Difference between
L
and
L/2
bounded

It is easy to see that for
p=0
,

C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})=0,

we will prove that for
p\in\{2/L,4/L,\ldots,1\}
,

|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})|\leq c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p)/L.

Then by (C1), for
p\in\{1/L,3/L,\ldots,1-1/L\}
,

\displaystyle|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L+c_{4}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (c_{4}+1)c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p)/L

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{5}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p)/L.

Suppose
p\in\{2/L,4/L,\ldots,1\}
,
we compare
C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p-\frac{1}{L})
and
C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p-\frac{1}{L})
. Intuitively, both of them are
\mathcal{O}(1/L)
, and their difference is
\mathcal{O}(1/L^{2})
.
In particular, both of them can be written into four parts:

\displaystyle\penalty\ C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p-\frac{1}{L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q)\right)C^{L}_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{L})

\displaystyle+\sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q)C^{L}_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\left(\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q)\right)\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{L})

\displaystyle+\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\int_{p-\frac{2}{L}}^{p}C^{L}_{t,s^{\prime},0}(q)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}^{L}(p,q)\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L})

\displaystyle+\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\int_{0}^{p-\frac{2}{L}}C^{L}_{t,s^{\prime},0}(q)(\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}^{L}(p,q)-\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}^{L}(p-\frac{2}{L},q))\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{4}^{L})

and
C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p-\frac{1}{L})=\mathcal{E}_{1}^{L/2}+\mathcal{E}_{2}^{L/2}+\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L/2}+\mathcal{E}_{4}^{L/2}
where

\mathcal{E}_{i}^{L/2}
is defined in the same way as
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{L}
but with
C^{L/2}
and
\Gamma^{L/2}
instead of
C^{L}
and
\Gamma^{L}
. Next we bound
|\mathcal{E}_{i}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{i}^{L/2}|
one by one:

1.

The only hard part to bound in
|\mathcal{E}_{i}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{i}^{L/2}|
is

|\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q)-(\Gamma^{L/2}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L/2}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q))|.

By almost the same proof of (A0),

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q)-(\Gamma^{L/2}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L/2}_{t,t^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{ct(c_{3}+c_{5}+ct+c_{4}+5)}{L^{2}}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L})).

Then we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{1}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{1}^{L/2}|/(2t(s+1))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{ct(c_{3}+c_{5}+ct+c_{4}+5)}{L^{2}}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))\cdot c\cdot c

\displaystyle+4ctc_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L\cdot c/L\cdot c

\displaystyle+4ctc_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L\cdot c\cdot c/L

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c^{3}t(c_{3}+c_{5}+ct+c_{4}+13)}{L^{2}}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))

2.

Bounding
|\mathcal{E}_{2}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{2}^{L/2}|
is similar to
|\mathcal{E}_{1}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{1}^{L/2}|
, where we first bound

|\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q)-(\Gamma^{L/2}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L/2}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q))|\leq 9c^{2}t/L^{2}.

Then we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{2}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{2}^{L/2}|/(2t(s+1))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{3}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))/L\cdot c\cdot 2c/L

\displaystyle+c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))\cdot c/L\cdot 2c/L

\displaystyle+c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))\cdot c\cdot 9c^{2}t/L^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c^{2}(2c_{3}+2+9ct)}{L^{2}}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L})).

3.

For
|\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L/2}|
, we first simplify

\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L/2}=\frac{2}{L}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}C_{t,s^{\prime},0}^{L/2}(p)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}^{L/2}(p,p),

and

\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L}=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}C_{t,s^{\prime},0}^{L}(p)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}^{L}(p,p)+C_{t,s^{\prime},0}^{L}(p-\frac{1}{L})\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}^{L}(p,p-\frac{1}{L}).

Again, we introduce an intermediate term

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{3}^{L}=\frac{2}{L}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}C_{t,s^{\prime},0}^{L}(p)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},0,0}^{L}(p,p).

Then we can bound

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L/2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L}-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{3}^{L}|+|\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{3}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{3}^{L/2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{t}{L}(c_{4}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))/L\cdot c+c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))\cdot c/L)

\displaystyle+\frac{2t}{L}(c_{5}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/Lc+c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))\cdot c/L)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{tc(c_{4}+1+2c_{5}+2)}{L^{2}}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L})).

4.

For
|\mathcal{E}_{4}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{4}^{L/2}|
, we use

|\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q)-(\Gamma^{L/2}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p,q)-\Gamma^{L/2}_{s,s^{\prime},0,b}(p-\frac{2}{L},q))|\leq 9c^{2}t/L^{2},

which is used in
|\mathcal{E}_{2}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{2}^{L/2}|
. Finally,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{4}^{L}-\mathcal{E}_{4}^{L/2}|/t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{4}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))/L\cdot 2c/L

\displaystyle+c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L}))\cdot 9c^{2}t/L^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c(2c_{4}+9ct)}{L^{2}}c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{2}{L})).

In sum,

\displaystyle|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p-\frac{1}{L})-C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})+C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p-\frac{1}{L})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c^{3}t(4ct+2c_{4}+14)+c^{2}(2+15ct)+tc(3c_{4}+5)+c(2c_{4}+9ct)}{L^{2}}\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{c^{3}t(4ct+2c_{4}+29)+tc(3c_{4}+14)+c(2c_{4}+2c)}{L^{2}}\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L})).

Therefore, since
c_{1}=c^{3}t(4ct+2c_{4}+29)+tc(3c_{4}+14)+c(2c_{4}+2c)
,

\displaystyle|C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})-C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p+\frac{1}{L})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |C^{L}_{t,s,0}(p-\frac{1}{L})-C^{L/2}_{t,s,0}(p-\frac{1}{L})|+c_{1}/L^{2}\cdot c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+c_{1}/L)c_{2}\exp(c_{1}(p-\frac{1}{L}))/L

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ c_{2}\exp(c_{1}p)/L.

Appendix D
Classification of Depthwise Parametrizations in Linear Case

We discuss the classification results on the linear residual networks with SGD training and give rigorous proofs for the claims in this simplified setting. Recall the linear residual networks:

\forall l\in[L],x^{l}=x^{l-1}+aL^{-\alpha}h^{l},

where
h^{l}=W^{l}x^{l}
, and the effective learning rate of
W^{l}
is
\eta n^{-1}L^{-\gamma}
. Without loss of generality, we assume
\eta=a=1
.

D.1
Initialization

At initialization, we have

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l-1}\rangle+L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}h^{l}_{0}\rangle,

where

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}h_{0}^{l}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{0}^{l-1}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{0}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu.

Since
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l-1}\rangle
is independent from
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{0}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
,
we have

\langle x_{0}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l}\rangle=\langle x_{0}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l-1}\rangle+L^{-2\alpha}\langle h^{l}_{0}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}h^{l}_{0}\rangle=\langle x_{0}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l-1}\rangle+L^{-2\alpha}\langle x_{0}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l-1}\rangle=(1+L^{-2\alpha})\langle x_{0}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l-1}\rangle.

Using this recursion, we can write

\langle x_{0}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{l}\rangle=(1+L^{-2\alpha})^{l}\langle x_{0}^{0}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{0}\rangle.

Therefore,
\langle x_{0}^{L}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{L}\rangle=\Theta(1)
iff
\alpha\geq 1/2
, otherwise
(1+L^{-2\alpha})^{L}\approx e^{L^{-2\alpha+1}}
explodes with large
L
.

A similar argument stands for
h^{l}_{0}
and
f_{0}
. Therefore, we have proved
7.1
.

Similarly, we can get the stability of the first backward pass, i.e.,
\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}=\Theta(1)
for
\alpha\geq 1/2
. Given
\alpha\geq 1/2
, we can also settle the size of
\widetilde{\delta}h_{0}
that

\widetilde{\delta}h_{0}^{l}=\Theta(L^{-\alpha}),

which implies

\Delta W_{1}^{l}=L^{-\gamma+\alpha}\cdot\widetilde{\delta}h_{0}^{l}\otimes x_{0}^{l-1}.

D.2
After the first step of gradient update

Now we look at the second forward pass, and assume the input is the same, i.e.,
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{0}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{0}^{0}\rangle
, we have

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle+L^{-\alpha}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\Delta W_{1}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle)

where
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\Delta W_{1}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=-L^{-\gamma}\tilde{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h_{0}^{l}\rangle}\langle x_{0}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}=-L^{-\gamma}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}\rangle\langle x_{0}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
, and
\tilde{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h_{0}^{l}\rangle}\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}L^{\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h_{0}^{l}\rangle
is the normalized version of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h_{0}^{l}\rangle
, which happens to equal to
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}\rangle
. By the definition of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
and
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
, we get a similar formula to the Depth-
\mu
P case:

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle+L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}\rangle\left(\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}-L^{-\gamma}\langle x_{0}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle\right).

Now we write
b^{l}=L^{\gamma}\frac{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle}{\partial\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}
and
c^{l}=-\langle x_{0}^{l-1}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle
, then

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle+L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+L^{-\alpha-\gamma}(b^{l}+c^{l})\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}\rangle.

By expanding
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l-1}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}\rangle+L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{t}^{l}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{L}\rangle+\sum_{m=l}^{L}L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{t}^{m}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
,
we have

\displaystyle\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\talloblong$}x_{1}^{l}\rangle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle+L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+L^{-\alpha-\gamma}(b^{l}+c^{l})\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{L}\rangle+\sum_{m=l+1}^{L}L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{m}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{0}\rangle+\sum_{m=1}^{l}L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m}x_{1}^{m-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\sum_{m=1}^{l}L^{-\alpha-\gamma}(b^{m}+c^{m})\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{L}\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=2}^{L}L^{-\alpha-\gamma}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{\min\{m-1,l\}}(b^{l^{\prime}}+c^{l^{\prime}})L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{m}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu.

(11)

Note the four terms in
eq.

11
are independent of each other.

Now it is easy to compute
c^{l}
because only the first two terms in
eq.

11
have correlation with
x_{0}^{l}
:

c^{l}=c^{l-1}(1+L^{-2\alpha})=\Theta(1)

with
\alpha\geq 1/2
.
For
b^{l}
, we have the following recursive formula:

b^{l+1}=L^{-2\alpha}\sum_{m=1}^{l}(b^{l}+c^{l})=\Theta(l\cdot L^{-2\alpha}).

Stable during training and nontrivial.

Finally, we can reason about the
\mathring{f}_{1}
(note
\mathring{f}_{0}=0
, so
\Delta\mathring{f}_{1}=\mathring{f}_{1}
), which indicates whether the parametrization is stable during the first step
29
29

29

We need
\Delta x
and
\Delta h
for stability, but they are similar to
\Delta\mathring{f}_{1}
.
, and whether the parametrization is nontrivial for the first step:

\mathring{f}_{1}=\langle nV\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{L}\rangle=\sum_{m=1}^{L}L^{-\alpha-\gamma}(b^{m}+c^{m})\chi_{0}=\Theta(L^{1-\alpha-\gamma}).

Therefore, we have proved
7.2
that the parametrization is stable during training iff
\alpha+\gamma\geq 1
, and is nontrivial iff
\alpha+\gamma\leq 1
.

Faithfulness.

Although there is no activation in the linear case, we still prove
7.3
to enlighten the proof of the general case.

At the initialization,
h_{0}^{l}
and
x_{0}^{l-1}
have the same size, therefore, faithfulness is equivalent to stability, which means it happens iff
\alpha\geq 1/2
.

During training, we can expand
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}h_{1}^{l}\rangle
in a similar way to
eq.

11
as

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}h_{1}^{l}\rangle=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+L^{-\gamma}(b^{l}+c^{l})\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{L}\rangle+\sum_{m=l+1}^{L}L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{m}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu\right)=\Theta(1+L^{-\gamma}).

Therefore, it is faithful iff
\gamma\geq 0
. It is equivalent to
\alpha\leq 1
because we have
\alpha+\gamma=1
.

Feature diversity exponent.

To simplify the analysis, we assume that
\epsilon L
is always an integer. We first expand
x_{1}^{l+\epsilon L}-x_{1}^{l}

\displaystyle\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\talloblong$}x_{1}^{l+\epsilon L}\rangle-\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l}\rangle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{m=l+1}^{l+\epsilon L}L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m}x_{1}^{m-1}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\sum_{m=l+1}^{l+\epsilon L}L^{-\alpha-\gamma}(b^{m}+c^{m})\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{L}\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=2}^{L}L^{-\alpha-\gamma}\sum_{l^{\prime}=\min\{m-1,l\}+1}^{\min\{m-1,l+\epsilon L\}}(b^{l^{\prime}}+c^{l^{\prime}})L^{-\alpha}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{m\top}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{m}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu.

With
\alpha+\gamma=1
, it is clear that the first term is
\Theta(L^{-\alpha}\sqrt{\epsilon L})=\Theta(\epsilon^{1/2}L^{-\alpha+1/2})
,
the second term has size
\Theta(\epsilon)
,
and the third term has size
\Theta(\sqrt{L}\cdot\epsilon L^{-\alpha})=\Theta(\epsilon L^{-\alpha+1/2})
. Therefore, there are only two cases here: if
\alpha=1/2
, the overall size is
\Theta(\epsilon^{1/2}+\epsilon)=\Theta(\epsilon^{1/2})
; if
\alpha>1/2
, the first and the third term vanish as
L\to\infty
, so the overall size is
\Theta(\epsilon)
. In sum, we have proved
7.4
and
7.5
.

Layerwise linearization.

7.6
is trivial in this simplified setting because layerwise linearization is always true for linear nets. To enlighten the proof of the general case, we recap that
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\Delta W_{1}^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle=L^{-\gamma}c^{l}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{0}^{l}\rangle=\Theta(L^{-\gamma})
, which is much smaller than
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{l}x_{1}^{l-1}\rangle=\Theta(1)
when
\gamma>0
. If there were an activation function, the linearization would bring an error of
o(L^{-\gamma})
in
h_{1}^{l}
, which means an error of
o(L^{-\gamma-\alpha})=o(L^{-1})
to
x_{1}^{l}
.

D.3
Beyond one step

The argument above is in general tracking the derivatives and covariance, in other words,
\Gamma
and
C
in the Depth-
\mu
P case.

Now we generalize
Lemma

4.3
, and obtain the following recursion for
\Gamma
and
C

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}\left(\frac{l}{L},q\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma_{t,r,0,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},q\right)+L^{1/2-\alpha}\mathbb{I}_{[(t=r)\wedge(b=0)\wedge(l=\lceil Lq\rceil)]}

\displaystyle+L^{-\alpha-\gamma}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,r,1,b}\left(\frac{l}{L},q\right)\left(L^{\gamma-1/2}\Gamma_{t,s,0,1}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},\frac{l}{L}\right)-C_{t,s,0}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right)\right).

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,1,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},q\right)=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma_{t,r,1,b}\left(\frac{l}{L},q\right)+L^{1/2-\alpha}\mathbb{I}_{[(t=r)\wedge(b=1)\wedge(l=\lceil Lq\rceil)]}

\displaystyle+L^{-\alpha-\gamma}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,r,0,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L},q\right)\left(L^{\gamma-1/2}\Gamma_{t,s,1,0}\left(\frac{l}{L},\frac{l}{L}\right)-C_{t,s,1}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right)\right).

C_{t,s,a}(p)=\sum_{t^{\prime}=-1}^{t}\sum_{s^{\prime}=-1}^{s}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\Gamma_{t,t^{\prime},a,b}(l/L,q)C_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},a,b}(l/L,q)\mathrm{d}q,

where
l=\lceil Lp\rceil-1
if
a=0
, and
l=\lceil Lp\rceil
if
a=1
.

Then all the claims can be reasoned by tracking the order of
\Gamma
and
C
.

Distinguish parametrizations with
\alpha+\gamma=1
and
\alpha\leq 1
.

The parametrizations with
\alpha+\gamma=1
and
\alpha\leq 1
are all nontrivial, stable, and faithful. However, there is a large gap between
\alpha=1/2
(Depth-
\mu
P) and
\alpha>1/2
in terms of the difficulty of tracking
\Gamma
and
C
. For
\alpha>1/2
, we can see that

C_{t,s,a}=\Theta(1)
,
\Gamma_{t,-1,a,b}=\Theta(1)
and
\Gamma_{t,s,a,b}=o(1)
for
s\geq 0
. In this case, we can simplify the recursion by ignoring
\Gamma_{t,s,a,b}
with
s\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,-1,0,b}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right)\approx

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma_{t,-1,0,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L}\right)-\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,-1,1,b}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right)C_{t,s,0}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right).

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,-1,1,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L}\right)\approx

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma_{t,-1,1,b}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right)-\frac{1}{L}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,-1,0,b}\left(\frac{l-1}{L}\right)C_{t,s,1}\left(\frac{l}{L}\right).

C_{t,s,a}(p)\approx\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\Gamma_{t,-1,a,b}(l/L)\Gamma_{s,-1,a,b}(l/L),

where
l=\lceil Lp\rceil-1
if
a=0
, and
l=\lceil Lp\rceil
if
a=1
. Note
\Gamma_{t,-1,a,b}(p,q)
is simplified to a function that only depends on
p
because
\Gamma_{t,-1,a,b}(p,q)
is constant when fixing
p
.

This simplification means the randomness in any
W_{0}^{l}
does not have an effect on the dynamics in the infinite depth limit — the complicated functional integrals for
\alpha=1/2
in
4.4
are simplified to be ODEs when
\alpha>1/2
. This ODE dynamic also directly implies that the feature diversity exponent is 0 for
\alpha>1/2
.

Appendix E
Nonlinear Depth-
\mu
P Limit

When the nonlinearity
\phi
is nontrivial, the distribution of the final representations
x^{L}_{s}
may be highly non-Gaussian because of complex compositions of
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
, as one would suspect from known examples of large width limits.
This is indeed the case for finite depth
L
. But in fact, when
L\to\infty
,
\{x^{L}_{s}\}_{s\geq 0}
becomes a Gaussian process again!

The kernel of the limiting GP can be computed in a similar way as in the linear case:

Definition E.1
.

Define
\Gamma
and
C
recursively by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,0,b}(p,q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}_{[(t=r)\wedge(b=0)\wedge(p\geq q)]}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{p}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}V_{\phi^{\prime}}[C]_{t,s,0}(p^{\prime})\Gamma_{s,r,1,b}(p^{\prime},q)\cdot(\Gamma_{t,s,0,1}(p^{\prime},p^{\prime})-C_{t,s,0}(p^{\prime}))\mathrm{d}p^{\prime};

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,r,1,b}(p,q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}_{[(t=r)\wedge(b=1)\wedge(p\leq q)]}

\displaystyle+\int_{p}^{1}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Gamma_{s,r,0,b}(p^{\prime},q)\cdot(V_{\phi_{c}|\phi^{\prime}}[C]_{t,s,0}\Gamma_{t,s,1,0}(p^{\prime},p^{\prime})-V_{\phi^{\prime}}[C]_{t,s,0}(p^{\prime})C_{t,s,1}(p^{\prime}))\mathrm{d}p^{\prime};

\displaystyle C_{t,s,a}(p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\int_{0}^{1}\Gamma_{t,t^{\prime},a,b}(p,q)V_{\phi_{c}}[C]_{t^{\prime},s^{\prime},b}(q)\Gamma_{s,s^{\prime},a,b}(p,q)\mathrm{d}q.

Here

\displaystyle V_{\phi_{c}}[C]_{t,s,b}(p)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}$}}\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\mathrm{MS}(\phi(z))\mathrm{MS}(\phi(y))

\displaystyle V_{\phi^{\prime}}[C]_{t,s,b}(p)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}$}}\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\phi^{\prime}(z)\phi^{\prime}(y)

\displaystyle V_{\phi_{c}|\phi^{\prime}}[C]_{t,s,b}(p)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}$}}\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\mathrm{MS}(\phi^{\prime}(z))\phi^{\prime}(y)

where
(z,y)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,C^{b}(p)|_{\{t,s\}})
.

Then

Claim E.1
.

For sufficiently smooth nonlinearity
\phi
, in the
L\to\infty
limit, the kets
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x^{L}_{s}\rangle,s=0,1,\ldots,
converge in distribution as a zero-mean Gaussian process with kernel

\langle x^{L}_{s}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x^{L}_{t}\rangle=C_{t,s,1}(1).

as defined in
Definition

E.1
.
Thus, for each fixed neuron index
\alpha
, the collection
\{x^{L}_{\alpha s}\}_{s\geq 0}
converges in distribution to a zero-mean Gaussian process with kernel
C_{t,s,1}(1)
in the
n\to\infty
then
L\to\infty
limit.

We frame this as a claim because we do not want to get into the details of what “sufficiently smooth nonlinearity” means here, nor do we give a proof. Instead, we give an intuitive justification.

Heuristic justification of
E.1
.

First, in Depth-
\mu
P, we can Taylor expand each block

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\phi(W^{l}_{t}x^{l-1}_{t})\rangle=\phi(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)+\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)[\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W^{l}_{t}x^{l-1}_{t}\rangle+\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu]+\mathcal{O}(L^{-1}).

The remainder term thus contributes
\mathcal{O}(L^{-3/2})
after accounting for the
L^{-1/2}
block multiplier. Summing over depth
l\in[0,L]
, by Gronwall’s lemma, the remainders of all layers sum up to
\mathcal{O}(L^{-1/2})
, so we can ignore them.
From here on, we study the linearized blocks
x\mapsto\mathrm{MS}(\phi(W^{l}_{0}x)+\phi^{\prime}(W^{l}_{0}x)\odot\bar{\Delta}W^{l}_{t}x)
.

Now the key observation is that each of
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x^{l}_{t}\rangle
or
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\delta x^{l}_{t}\rangle
is always equal, up to
\mathcal{O}(L^{-1/2})
factor, to a linear combination of
\{\mathrm{MS}\phi(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{s}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu),\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l\top}_{0}\delta h^{l}_{s}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu\}_{l,s}
, where each coefficient in this linear combination is
\mathcal{O}(L^{-1/2})
and deterministic.
We call such a linear combination a
good linear combination
.
One can see this by an induction argument on
t
.
Indeed, at initialization
t=0
, this claim is trivially true.
Supposing this claim is true for
t
, then it is trivial to see that it remains true at
t+1
for the backward pass kets
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\delta x^{l}_{t}\rangle
.
The only nontrivial part is to show the forward pass for
t+1
.
By the Taylor expansion above, we just need to show that
\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)[\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W^{l}_{t}x^{l-1}_{t}\rangle+\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu]
is “well-approximated” by a good linear combination.
By induction, both
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W^{l}_{t}x^{l-1}_{t}\rangle
and
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu
are of products between a good linear combination and a term of form
\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{m}_{0}x^{m-1}_{s}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)
for some
s\leq t
. Thus
\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)[\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W^{l}_{t}x^{l-1}_{t}\rangle+\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu]
is of the form
\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{m}_{0}x^{m-1}_{s}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)
times a good linear combination.
But
\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{m}_{0}x^{m-1}_{s}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)
only correlates with a single component in this linear combination and is independent with all other components.
Therefore, when summing over depth, the noncorrelated components will experience law of large numbers and one can replace
\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{l}_{0}x^{l-1}_{t}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W^{m}_{0}x^{m-1}_{s}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)
with its expectation; the correlated components is only a sum of
\mathcal{O}(L)
elements each of size
\mathcal{O}(L^{-3/2})
, so in total they are
o(1)
.
This completes the induction.

In this reasoning, the coefficients of a good linear combination correspond to
\Gamma
in
Definition

E.1
, and filling in the details of the induction yields the recursive formula in
Definition

E.1
.

Finally, because a good linear combination is a large sum of independent terms, the central limit theorem tells us that
\{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x^{l}_{t}\rangle,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\delta x^{l}_{t}\rangle\}_{l,t}
converge in distribution to a Gaussian process.
∎

E.1
is good news for theorists, that we have such a simple form for a fundamental architecture.
At the same time, one may worry that this Gaussian form lacks expressivity.
But in fact, some common architecture or algorithm choices would make the limit non-Gaussian.
For example, the use of adaptive optimizers like Adam or SignSGD.
Or the addition of a nonlinearity before the matrix multiply, i.e., “prenonlin”, (in addition to the one afterward, “postnonlin”).

In general, one obtains a stochastic differential equation with McKean-Vlasov elements describing the evolution of
x^{l}_{t}
over depth and time. However, the stochastic integral involved is
not
the usual Ito or Stratonovich integral because the depthwise evolution requires integrating non-adapted process against Brownian motion. Instead, we need to use
Skorohod
integral and the SDE is only defined in the sense of Malliavin calculus.
This is not just a mathematical nitpick; rather, assuming Ito calculus (which amounts to assuming incorrect independence between certain quantities) will lead to the wrong predictions and calculations.
Malliavin calculus is intimately connected to Tensor Programs and we shall develop their relationship as well as the theory of the general infinite-depth limit in a future work.

Appendix F
Heuristics for the proofs in the general case

The notation in this section is mostly defined in
appendix

A
. The complete notation is defined in
[
24
]
.

F.1
Depth-
\mu
P

Let
\mathrm{MS}(x)=x-\langle x,1\rangle/n=Gx
where
G=I-11^{\top}/n
,
where
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Recall the definition of the network and the normalized gradients

\displaystyle x^{1}

\displaystyle=U\xi

\displaystyle h^{l}

\displaystyle=W^{l}x^{l-1}

\displaystyle x^{l}

\displaystyle=x^{l-1}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}G\phi(h^{l})

\displaystyle f(\xi)

\displaystyle=V^{\top}x^{L}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}x^{L}

\displaystyle=nV

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}h^{l}

\displaystyle=\phi^{\prime}(h^{l})\odot(G\widetilde{\delta}x^{l})

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}x^{l-1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\delta}x^{l}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}W^{l\top}\widetilde{\delta}h^{l}

where
V=\Theta(1/n)
coordinatewise,
\widetilde{\delta}x^{l}=\Theta(1)
coordinatewise
and
W^{l}=\Theta(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})
coordinate-wise.

We also abuse the notation of
G
and use it as an operator on kets:
G\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle\mathbin{\overset{\text{{def}}}{=}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle-\mathbb{E}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x\rangle
.

Forward.

Similar to the linear case, one can show that under technical conditions (mostly on the activation function) that the infinite-depth limit of the TP follows the dynamics

\displaystyle d\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d\lambda}G\phi\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle+\sqrt{d\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d\lambda}G\phi\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)+d\lambda G\phi^{\prime}\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d\lambda}G\phi\left(\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle+\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)+d\lambda G\phi^{\prime}\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d\lambda}G\phi\left(\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)+d\lambda G\phi^{\prime}\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)\left(\widetilde{\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle}+\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)

where
\lambda\in[0,1]
refers to the fractional layer index (
\lambda
represents layer index
\lfloor\lambda L\rfloor
as
L\to\infty
),
t
refers to the training step,
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}
the matrix operator (defined in
Appendix

B
), and the tilde symbol refers to the “normalized” version of the object, i.e., multiply the ket with
(d\lambda)^{c}
for some
c
such that the multiplication (normalized ket) is
\Theta(1)
w.r.t.
L
, and same for the normalized operators. We also simplify
\widetilde{\delta}
to
\delta
if it is already under wider tilde symbol. The first term represents a Gaussian noise.

In the linear case, we have

d\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle=\sqrt{d\lambda}\left(\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)+d\lambda\left(\widetilde{\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle}+\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)

Note

\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle=\sqrt{d\lambda}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\widetilde{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\delta h_{s}^{\lambda}\rangle}\langle\nabla_{W_{0}^{\lambda\top}\widetilde{\delta}h_{s}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle=\sqrt{d\lambda}\widetilde{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\delta\bm{h}_{<t}^{\lambda}\rangle}\check{\langle}W_{0}^{\lambda\top}\widetilde{\delta}\bm{h}_{<t}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

Using multi-vector notation, we write

\displaystyle\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\talloblong$}\widetilde{\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\eta\widetilde{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\delta\bm{h}_{<t}^{\lambda}\rangle}_{\bm{\chi}}\langle\bm{x}_{<t}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle=-\eta\sum_{s<t}\widetilde{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\delta h_{s}^{\lambda}\rangle}_{\chi_{s}}\langle x_{s}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\talloblong$}\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\eta\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}\bm{h}_{<t}^{\lambda}\rangle_{\bm{\chi}}\langle\bm{x}_{<t}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle=-\eta\sum_{s<t}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}h_{s}^{\lambda}\rangle}_{\chi_{s}}\langle x_{s}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

Backward.

Similar to the forward prop, we obtain the following dynamics for the infinite-depth TP

\displaystyle-d\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{\tau}^{\lambda\top}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\phi^{\prime}(W_{\tau}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda})\odot(G\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda})\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d\lambda}\left(\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda\top}\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}+\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda\top}\dot{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}+\sqrt{d\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}\widetilde{W_{\tau}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}^{\dagger}\right)\left[\phi^{\prime}\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\sqrt{d\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{W_{0}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}}\dot{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu+\sqrt{d\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\bar{\Delta}W_{\tau}^{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}G\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d\lambda}\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda\top}\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}\left[\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern
