\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\in\sigma(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U_{0},V_{0}\rangle)\otimes\sigma(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U_{0},V_{0}\rangle)
,

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\in\sigma(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U_{0},V_{0}\rangle)

and the expectation is really just over

Forward

\displaystyle d\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=(d\lambda)^{\alpha}G\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi\left(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle+(d\lambda)^{1-\alpha}\widetilde{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)\mid\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U_{0},V_{0}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=d\lambda\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi^{\prime}\left(\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}W_{0}^{\lambda}\widehat{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle\right)\right]G\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{t}^{\lambda}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}x_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle

because the same reason as above.

F.3.2
Backward

\displaystyle-d\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\rangle

\displaystyle=d\lambda\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{\tau}^{\lambda\top}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\phi^{\prime}(W_{\tau}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda})\odot(G\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda})\rangle\mid\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U_{0},V_{0}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=d\lambda\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{\tau}^{\lambda\top}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\phi^{\prime}(W_{\tau}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda})\odot(G\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda})\rangle\mid\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}U_{0},V_{0}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=d\lambda\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)\right]\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{\tau}^{\lambda\top}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}G\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\rangle

Here,

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\phi^{\prime}(W_{\tau}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda})\odot(G\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda})\rangle\equiv\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)\right]G\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\rangle+(d\lambda)^{1-\alpha}\operatorname*{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi^{\prime\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}W_{0}^{\lambda}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\widehat{\rangle\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu)\right]G\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\bar{\Delta}W_{\tau}^{\lambda\top}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\displaystyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\textstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptstyle\talloblong$}}{\scalebox{0.7}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\talloblong$}}\widetilde{\delta}x_{\tau}^{\lambda}\rangle

F.4
Justifications of the claims

Claim
7.2
.
Stability during training when
\alpha+\gamma\geq 1
is straightforward (some technical conditions on the activation function are required). This is because the weight updates are of order
L^{-\alpha-\gamma}
and feature updates involve no more than
L
terms of size
L^{-\alpha-\gamma}
(plus higher order terms that do not contribute to the update in the large depth limit). When
\alpha+\gamma>1
, the contribution of a sum of at most
L
terms of order
L^{-\alpha-\gamma}
will decrease to zero, and the network output
f_{t}
will converge to
f_{0}
in this case, yielding a trivial limit. However, when
\alpha+\gamma=1
, the updates remain important in the infinite depth limit, yielding a non-trivial limit.

Claim
7.3
.
Consider a stable and nontrivial parametrization (i.e.
\alpha+\gamma=1
). Faithfulness at initialization is achieved only when
\alpha\geq 1/2
. This was proven in
[
7
]
in a more general setup. Faithfulness during training is ensured as long as
\alpha\leq 1
because feature updates are always
\Theta(1)
in depth. With
\alpha>1
,
\gamma<0
and the weight updates explode with depth in this case, which yield exploding behaviour for
\bm{h}
.

Claim
7.4
When
\alpha\in(1/2,1]
, we obtain smooth limiting dynamics when
L\to\infty
as demonstrated in
Section

F.3
. This limiting process is a smooth process (no Brownian jumps) that satisfies the required definition of redundancy.

Claim
7.5
.
It remains to prove that Depth-
\mu
P is non-redundant. This is a result of the limiting dynamics in this case (
Section

F.1
) . With Depth-
\mu
P, the randomness of the initialization in the hidden layer remains present throughout training, inducing a Brownian-like term that breaks redundancy.

Claim
7.6
.
In Depth-
\mu
P,
W_{t}^{l}-W_{0}^{l}
is
\Theta(1/\sqrt{L})
which is much smaller than
W_{0}^{l}
. Therefore,
\phi(W^{l}_{t}\bm{x}_{t}^{l-1})-\phi(W^{l}_{0}\bm{x}_{t}^{l-1})-\phi^{\prime}(W_{0}^{l}\bm{x}_{t}^{l-1})\odot((W_{t}^{l}-W_{0}^{l})\bm{x}_{t}^{l-1})=o(1/\sqrt{L})
, thus satisfies
7.7
.
Similar to the depth-
\mu
P case, for
\alpha\in[1/2,1)
, the activation in the forward pass can be linearized which indicates layerwise linearization when
\alpha+\gamma=1
.

Appendix G
Additional Experiments

G.1
Failure of Standard Parametrization at Large Depths

Figure 20:
Training with Standard Parametrization fails at large depth due to numerical issues.

G.2
Experiments with Block Depth 2

Currently, our theory covers resnets with block depth 1, and our experiments confirm the theoretical findings. We conducted similar experiments for clock depth 2 (i.e. the residual block consists of 2 fully connected layers) to see whether the learning rate transfers with Depth-
\mu
P. The results are reported in
Figure

21
. The results show a significant shift in the learning rate which might indicate that as block depth increases, adjustments are needed to stabilize hyperparameters with depth.

Figure 21:
Same setup as
Figure

10
, with block depth 2 instead.
